115 年 8 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 9.4%，較上年
同月增 41.0%(按新臺幣計算增 52.6%)；累計 1 至 8 月出口 5,743.4
億美元，較上年同期增 44.2%(按新臺幣計算增 46.5%)。 
（二）進口 
8 月進口 601.0 億美元，為歷年單月第 3 高，較上月增 3.4%，較上
年同月增 44.3%(按新臺幣計算增 56.2%)；累 計1 至 8 月進口 4,374.3
億美元，較上年同期增 40.4%(按新臺幣計算增 42.4%)。 
（三）出入超 
8 月出超 223.0 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 55.2 億美元；
累計 1 至 8 月出超 1,369.1 億美元，較上年同期增 500.5 億美元。

### Page 2

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用持續擴增，相關
產品出貨維持強勁， 加上價格攀升效 應，8月出口較上年同月增 239.7
億美元(+41.0%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 電子零組件出口 322.2 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增118.3
億美元(+58.0%)，其中積體電路增 115.5 億美元(+60.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+29.9%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.7 億美元(+24.9%)。 
2. 資通與視聽產品出口 331.0 億美元，較上年同月增 97.6 億美元
(+41.8%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增68.7 億美元(+40.4%)、儲存媒
體增 19.1 億美元(+2.4 倍)、 交換器及路由器增9.7 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 礦產品出口 20.0 億美元，較上年同月增 8.3 億美元(+70.2%)，其
中石油煉製品增 7.8 億美元(+74.3%)。 
4. 基本金屬及其製品出口 29.6 億美元，較上年同月增 5.4 億美元
(+22.4%)，其中銅及其製品增 6.1 億美元(+1.5 倍)，鋼鐵及其製品
減 1.1 億美元(-8.7%)。 
5. 機械出口 26.8 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+15.2%)，其中
生產半導體等機械增 1.9 億美元(+42.1%)、未列名具特殊功能機械
增 0.4 億美元(+39.0%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月出口較上年同期增 1,759.8 億美元(+44.2%)，以資通與
視聽產品增 1,023.7 億美元(+68.5%)、電子零組件增 629.5 億美元
(+45.8%)增加較多。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路及資通產
品進口維持強勁，加上輸入物價明顯上漲，8 月總進口較上年同月增
184.5 億美元(+44.3%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口408.4 億美元， 較上年同月增129.4億美元(+46.4%)，
其中電子零組件增 82.5 億美元(+62.6%)、礦產品增 16.9 億美元
(+38.4%)、電腦之零附件增 14.6 億美元(+84.4%)、基本金屬增 4.5
億美元(+21.8%)、塑化原料增 4.0 億美元(+16.2%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 130.6 億美元， 較上年同月增 38.4 億美元(+41.6%)，
其中資通設備增 27.2 億美元(+1.2 倍)、半導體設備增 2.8 億美元
(+7.3%)、交通運輸設備增 2.8 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 55.7 億美元，較上年同月增 16.0 億美元(+40.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增12.1 億美元(+3.7 倍)、藥粧用品增 1.2
億美元(+17.4%)、手機增 1.1 億美元(+78.1%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月對陸港、日本出口創新高，對美國、東協為單月第 3 高 
1. 對美國出口 236.7 億美元，較上年同月增 40.6 億美元(+20.7%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 23.0 億美元(+14.6%)、電子零組件增 11.6
億美元(+1.5 倍)、電機產品增 1.7 億美元(+30.1%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 211.5 億美元，較上年同月增 59.7 億美元
(+39.3%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+45.6%)、資通與視聽
產品增 6.7 億美元(+42.9%)、 基本金屬及其製品增4.6 億美元(+1.2
倍)。 
3. 對東協出口 151.3 億美元，較上年同月增 44.9 億美元(+42.2%)，
其中電子零組件增 25.3 億美元(+50.3%)、資通與視聽產品增 12.6
億美元(+41.1%)、礦產品增 4.6 億美元(+1.5 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 44.5 億美元，較上年同月增 11.5 億美元(+34.8%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 6.6 億美元(+57.0%)、電子零組件增 4.0 億美
元(+1.1 倍)，基本金屬及其製品減 1.4 億美元(-31.0%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.6 億美元，較上年同月增8.9 億美元(+32.0%)，其中
電子零組件增 8.9 億美元(+76.7%)、 化學品增0.5 億美元(+32.3%)，
資通與視聽產品減 0.6 億美元(-13.9%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月對美國出口 1,803.2 億美元，較上年同期增 631.6 億美
元(+53.9%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,394.7 億美元，增 307.5 億美
元(+28.3%)， 對東協出口1,150.7 億美元 ， 增372.3 億美元(+47.8%)，
對歐洲出口 376.6 億美元 ， 增130.7 億美元(+53.1%)， 對日本出口247.0
億美元，增 54.7 億美元(+28.4%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月自中國大陸與香港進口 126.8 億美元 ， 較上年同月增47.6 億美元
(+60.2%)，自東協進口 106.9 億美元，增46.7 億美元(+77.6%)，自南
韓進口 91.7 億美元，增29.6 億美元(+47.7%)，自日本進口 57.0 億美
元，增 11.2 億美元(+24.4%)，自美國進口 53.9 億美元，增 14.3 億美
元(+35.9%)，自歐洲進口 49.5 億美元，增 6.2 億美元(+14.3%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增75.4%、
自南韓增 63.2%、自中國大陸與香港增 40.5%、自美國增 24.4%、自
日本增 24.1%、自歐洲增 12.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-7 月主要國家/地區外貿景氣普遍上升，南韓(1-8 月)、香港、
新加坡出口動能尤為強勁，分別年增 52.8%、46.6%、38.3%；中國
大陸(1-8 月)、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-6 月)、日
本各年增 10.9%、8.1%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-7 月以香港進口年增 48.1%最
為顯著， 新加坡、 中國大陸(1-8 月)分別年增 37.0%、27.0%，南韓(1-8
月)、德國(1-6 月)各增 18.7%、11.7%，日本、美國各增 6.5%、1.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）得益於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及 雲端服務等應用 需求強勁， 加
以外銷產品價格攀升，8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，年
增 41.0%，連續 34 個月正成長；進口 601.0 億美元，年增 44.3%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求擴張 ，及農工原料行情上
漲；出、進口互抵後，出超 223.0 億美元，亦創單月新高 。累計 1
至 8 月出口年增 44.2%、進口增 40.4%。
（二）8 月出口主要貨品以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品貢獻最多，年增率
分別為 58.0%及 41.8%，前者受惠 AI 商機及記憶體外銷價量齊揚，規
模值創單月新高，兩類併計增 49.3%，其餘貨類平均增 16.3%，以礦
產品隨油品售價上漲，年增 70.2%較為突出，基本金屬及其製品、電
機產品各增 22.4%、22.3%。累計 1 至 8 月資通與視聽產品出口值已
超越歷年水準，與電子零組件合計占總出口比重達 78.7%。

（三）8 月對主要市場出口同呈雙位數成長，其中對中國大陸與香港、日本
受電子零組件外銷強勁推升，出口值同創單月新高，各年增 39.3%、
32.0%，對東協、歐洲、美國分別增 42.2%、34.8%、20.7%；五大市
場之外，對墨西哥及澳大利亞出口皆增逾 2 倍。累計 1 至 8 月對美
國、歐洲出口各年增 53.9%、53.1%，對東協年增 47.8%，對日本、
陸港各增 28.4%、28.3%，對五大市場出口規模均為歷年同期最佳。
（四）展望未來，地緣政治衝突 及美國關稅政策變化等不確定 因素，持續
干擾全球經濟成長力道，惟隨人工智慧應用 不斷深化，算力需求攀
升，國際雲端服務業者加速建置 AI 基礎設施，加以國內半導體及伺
服器等供應鏈具競爭優勢，均有助支撐我國外銷動能，下半年出口
可望維持成長態勢。

### Page 1

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co.

TSMC-Musk: customer relationship intact; 
tech/control sharing with Terafab unlikely

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 3,100 TWD | Price: 2,500 TWD

Customer neutrality central to TSMC’s foundry model 
Elon Musk on Saturday October 3rd responded “Just discussions, but something may come 
of it” to an article discussing the potential manufacturing partnership between TSMC 
and Terafab. From chipset design and foundry service perspective, we expect a deeper 
collaboration as Tesla and SpaceX grow at TSMC (2-3% of its sales) directly with their 
auto/robotics and cloud AI ASICs and indirectly through merchant HPC chipset buys (e.g. 
GPUs, networking switches) based on our estimate. However, the likelihood of TSMC 
licensing technology or operating a fab with Terafab holding a meaningful share should 
be low. Specifically, we would note TSMC’s JV fab record is limited including WaferTech, 
SSMC, VIS, JASM, ESMC and AVSM mostly on mature nodes. The company did not co-
own a fab with Apple who contributed ~21% of its revenue from ’15-25 mostly on the 
advanced processes as it keeps full business control and preserves customer neutrality. 
Terafab capex insufficient for 1 terawatt capacity target 
Based on Terafab’s applications under Texas’s JETI program, it outlined ~US$119bn of 
planned investment across 4 phases from ’26-36, with ~60% of it on tool procurement. 
The spending in ’26-28 would be highly skewed toward facility construction before it 
starts to pull in US$6-8bn equipment per annum from ‘29-36. Based on our estimate of 
US$600mn+ for every 1k WPM expansion on the advanced nodes in the US, Terafab 
could only grow its capacity installed base by ~10k WPM per annum at maximum even if 
it can successfully deliver the technology roadmap through its own R&D effort and Intel 
partnership. This would prevent Terafab’s operation from the economies of scale as the 
node migration continues, keeping TSMC as the major foundry partner for Tesla/SpaceX. 
Stock at mid-cycle supported by robust AI demand pipeline 
We keep our Buy rating on TSMC with PO at NT$3,100 on 20x '27 P/E (vs. current 16x).

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,100 per share (US$590 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 18x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-40% 5-year CAGR 
in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

Chroma ATE
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,500 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG. Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical
range as its growth outlook is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its revenue is now AI-
related, with strong growth across product segments including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and should be well positioned to capitalize on the global AI expansion.

Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth expectation due to changes in capex outlook or
production bottleneck; 2) fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate improves; 3) disappointing
metrology shipment due to changes at ramp-up schedule; 4) testing process change leading to loss in market share; 5) FX risks
(50-60% of Chroma’s sales are in US$, 10-20% in Rmb).

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We believe an improving product mix, driven by rising 
Nvidia’s CPU contribution, to be a key near-term share price catalyst.

What’s the impact: Near-term update. After Kinsus released its August 
preliminary results on 23 September, we note that we had underestimated 
the 3Q26E gross margin expansion on product optimisation and price hike 
scale. The results imply a GM of 33% in August (vs. 26% in 2Q26). We 
therefore lift our 3Q-4Q26E gross margin to 33.2-35.1% (from 28.9-30.3%) 
to reflect an improving product mix, including greater CPU substrate 
contribution. We believe the gross margin of high-end chipset substrate 
may be c.50%, supporting margin expansion. We also expect a c.10% QoQ 
price hike for both BT and ABF substrate in 3Q26, above Kinsus’ guidance 
of a high-single-digit% QoQ, making these substrates the other growth 
driver. We see further gross margin expansion in 2027-28 on the ongoing 
price hike trend and increasing high-end ABF substrate product mix.

Business outlook. We believe several favourable trends lie ahead. First, 
we see a rising contribution from high-end GPU and CPU ABF substrates. 
We believe Nvidia’s CPU currently accounts for 5-10% of total revenue, 
with Kinsus holding a 70-80% market share. We also expect Kinsus to 
acquire a teens% market share in AMD’s Venice, which should gradually 
ramp up from 4Q26. These will likely become key growth drivers of the ABF 
substrate segment in 2027, in our view. We believe the ramp-up progress 
for GPU substrate for these two clients is slightly slower than that for CPU. 
Second, we believe Kinsus will benefit from the rising BT substrate demand 
for optical transceivers. In our view, migration to on-board optics (OBO) and 
NPO/CPO will require ABF substrate beneath the optics engine (OE) in 
OBO; the OE and IC will need a bigger ABF substrate as we move to the 
NPO/CPO generation. As such, we expect the total ABF substrate demand 
to increase with rising adoption of optics in AI servers. Lastly, we expect the 
adoption of multiple-core ABF from 2028 to improve the rigidity and 
transmission performance before the official launch of ceramic and glass-
core substrate, which we expect to increase the ABF TAM as well.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 19-29% to 
reflect the better-than-expected gross margin in August. We reaffirm our 
Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP to TWD1,580, based on an unchanged 
target PER of 36x (high end of 3-year-trading range of 10-36x), applied to 
the 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risks: weaker-than-
expected demand for memory, PCs, gaming GPUs or AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 27-57% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish view on its gross margin expansion.

6 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: several favourable trends ahead

➢ Rising high-end ABF substrate likely to drive blended ASP increase   
➢ Multicore and optics likely to be the next growth drivers 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD1,580

Company profile  
Kinsus was founded in 2000 as a subsidiary of Asustek. The company mainly focus on niche ball grid 
array (BGA) substrates to avoid price competition among mainstream products like graphics card and 
chipsets, and it has become top-3 flip chip manufacturer in global. Besides, its manufacturing sites are 
based in Taiwan and mainland China.

Kinsus Interconnect Technology (3189 TT): 6 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 31 Jan 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Kinsus's leadership team comprises experienced professionals dedicated to steering the
company towards sustainable growth. This diverse board composition ensures a comprehensive
range of expertise and perspectives, contributing to Kinsus’s strategic decision-making and
corporate governamce.
Capital management 2
Kinsus maintains a robust financial framework, ensuring the availability of resources necessary
for operational efficiency and strategic investments. The company's financial health is regularly
disclosed through annual reports, providing transparency to stakeholders.
Related party &
transaction 1
The company adheres to stringent corporate governance policies to manage related party
transactions, ensuring transparency and compliance with regulatory standards. The company
discloses such transactions in its financial reports, maintaining accountability and stakeholder
trust. For instance, the company's annual general meeting documents provide details on related
party transactions, ensuring transparency and accountability.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Kinsus is committed to sustainable supply chain practices, emphasizing environmental, social,
and governance considerations. The company has established comprehensive ESG policies,
including commitments to labor standards, environmental health and safety, ethics, and conflict-
free minerals. These policies guide the company's interactions with suppliers and partners,
promoting sustainability throughout the supply chain.
E Data security 3
The company recognizes the importance of information security in its operations. As part of its
corporate governance framework, Kinsus is likely to implement standard data protection
practices to safeguard sensitive information and ensure compliance with relevant regulations.
Stakeholders are encouraged to consult the company's official communications or contact the
company directly for detailed information on data security policies.

3Q26/4Q26 ABF、BT 稼動率持續向上，預期帶動營運成長 
南電 3Q26 主要成長動能來自於 ABF/BT，ABF 受惠 1.6T 高階交換器晶片及
AI ASIC 專案陸續放量，高階產品規格升級亦帶動平均銷售單價提升，其中
1.6T 交換器載板 ASP 相較前一代 800G 產品高出一倍以上。BT 載板則持續
受惠記憶體需求強勁。樹林廠及錦興廠的 ABF 與 BT 載板產能均已接近全面
滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率 60%–70%以上，進入 4Q26 有望趨於滿載。本中心
預估 3Q/4Q26 營收 163.8/188.5 億元，季增 20.6%/15.1% ，毛利率
30%/32.3%，稅後獲利 35/46.3 億元，EPS 5.43/7.16 元。 
玻纖布、CCL 持續漲價，預期 4Q26–2027 年載板價格上漲 
經本中心供應鏈訪查了解，高階 BT 材料已呈明顯供不應求，使部分 BT 料
件 Lead time 甚至拉長至 70 週以上；ABF 隨新世代 AI 載板朝 20 層以上
Build-up 結構發展，單顆 ABF 膜使用量同步提高，Ajinomoto 供應亦趨緊；
玻纖布方面， Nittobo 新增玻纖布供給已大致完成客戶分配，短期缺口難以
明顯緩解。隨單顆載板面積放大，每張 Panel 可排入的產品數量將快速下降，
持續消耗載板廠商有效產能。綜合來看，本輪載板漲價由規格升級、單顆材
料用量增加與高階有效產能不足共同驅動，預期 ABF/BT 載板於 4Q26 至
2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。 
2027 年營運動能明顯增強，給予買進評等，目標價 1,700 元 
南電於 8M26 董事會通過 468 億元資本支出計畫，主要鎖定大尺寸、高層數、
高精密度 IC 載板。在高階產品比重提升、稼動率維持高檔及報價上調帶動
下，預期 ABF/BT 毛利率可望持續改善，整體營運動能於 2027 年明顯增強。
本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1,095.4 億元，年增 49.3%/82.6%，
毛利率 26.9%/43.4%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。本中心給予南電買進評等，以
2027 年 EPS，30 倍本益比推得目標價 1,700 元。

元大觀點 
⧫  
台灣載板產能接近全面滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率提升至 60%–70%且
4Q26 有望滿載，預估 3Q/4Q26 營收季增 20.6%/15.1%。 
⧫  
規格升級與高階 產品及上游原料 產能不足共同驅動載板漲價，預期
ABF/BT 載板於 4Q26 至 2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。 
⧫  
高階產品比重提升、稼 動率維持高檔及報價上調，給予 南電 買進評
等，目標價 1,700 元，以 2027 年 EPS 56.78 元及 30 倍本益比推估。

營運分析 
3Q26 ABF、BT 稼動率持續向上，預期帶動營運成長 
南電 3Q26 營收 163.8 億元，季增 20.6%，年增 49.3%，主要成長動能仍來自於 ABF/BT，ABF 載
板方面，受惠 1.6T 高階交換器晶片及 AI ASIC 專案陸續放量，除出貨量持續增加外，高階產品規
格升級亦帶動平均銷售單價提升。其中，1.6T 交換器載板 ASP 相較前一代 800G 產品高出一倍以
上，隨高階產品占比提升，為營收與獲利成長的推力。BT 載板則持續受惠 DRAM 與 NAND Flash
等記憶體需求強勁，加上上游原材料成本上漲逐步反映於產品報價，使 BT 載板呈現量價齊揚。毛
利率預估為 30%，營業利益 43.7 億元，季增 50.6%，年增 604.1%，稅後獲利 35 億元，季增 55%，
年增 383.6%，EPS 5.43 元。

台灣廠全面滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率快速回升 
產能利用率方面，目前台灣主要載板廠區維持高檔運作，其中樹林廠及錦興廠的 ABF 與 BT 載板產
能均已接近全面滿載，顯示高階載板及記憶體相關需求仍相當強勁。同時，昆山 ABF 廠則為後續
營運改善的重要來源。隨美系網通大廠新專案自 7M26 正式進入量產，昆山 ABF 稼動率已由 2Q26
約 50%快速提升至 60%–70%，進入 4Q26 有望趨於滿載。伴隨稼動率提高及良率改善，昆山廠毛
利率亦顯著提升，預期後續貢獻幅度將提升。PCB 業務，稼動率正由 70%–75%向上，主因 AI 伺
服器板與記憶體模組板需求驅動。綜合上述，預期 4Q26 營收 188.5 億元，季增 15.1%，年增
68.8%，毛利率 32.3%，營業利益 55.1 億元，季增 26.1%，年增 472.1%，稅後獲利 46.3 億元，
季增 32%，EPS 7.16 元

玻纖布、 CCL 等價格仍持續漲價，預期 4Q26-2027 年載板價格上漲 
隨 AI GPU 、ASIC、高速交換器及 HBM 等應用快速成長，IC 載板規格持續朝大尺寸、高層數、細
線路、低翹曲及高耐熱發展，帶動材料由一般電子級產品轉向特種玻纖布、高階 BT 樹脂、ABF 增
層膜等高規材料。經本中心近期供應鏈訪查及日本材料展資訊整理，三菱瓦斯化學（ MGC）指出，
過去 BT 載板主要應用於 DRAM、NAND Flash 及消費性 IC，但隨 HBM、AI ASIC 、網通晶片與
CPO 導入，材料須進一步滿足低 CTE、高耐熱及高速電性需求，高階 BT 與傳統產品的規格差距
持續擴大，目前高階 BT 材料已呈明顯供不應求，部分料件 Lead time 甚至拉長至 70 週以上。玻纖
布方面，Nittobo 目前產能維持 100%滿載，雖規劃 2027–2028 年新增產能，但新增供給已大致完
成客戶分配，短期缺口難以明顯緩解。ABF 方面，隨新世代 AI 載板朝 20 層以上 Build-up 結構發
展，單顆 ABF 膜使用量同步提高，Ajinomoto 供應亦趨緊；同時 Resonac 相關載板材料報價已調
升約 20%–30%。綜合來看，本輪載板漲價並非單純景氣循環，而是規格升級、單顆材料用量增加
與高階有效產能不足共同驅動，預期 ABF／BT 載板於 4Q26 至 2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第4 頁，共 19頁  
圖 3：載板面積及層數增加亦在 Ajinomoto 未來載板發展藍圖

資料來源：Ajinomoto，元大投顧整理

南電此前已宣布 468 億元資本支出，為創立以來單筆最大投資 
南電於 8M26 董事會通過新一輪資本支出計畫，總投資金額達 468 億元，為公司成立以來最大單筆
投資案，規模亦超過 2018–2022 年上一輪擴產期間累計資本支出。其中約 110 億元投入廠房土建，
358 億元用於設備採購，新廠定位為具備 Class 100 無塵室等級的高階智慧工廠，主要鎖定大尺寸、
高層數、高精密度 IC 載板，以及 CoWoS、CoPoS 等先進封裝衍生需求。顯示此次投資並非既有
產線單純擴充，而是針對下一世代高階載板重新建置生產平台。整體而言 ，廠區調配方面，錦興廠
P2 升級已於 1H26 完成，主要提升自動化程度與無塵室規格，以因應大型、高階載板生產；樹林
廠則因應 2027 年美系客戶 Server CPU 需求成長，陸續將部分既有成熟產品轉往昆山生產，以釋
放台灣高階產能供新產品使用。

展望 2026-2027 年，ABF/BT 料源及載板的產能供應缺口將推升載板廠商營運 
AI 需求正在從「訓練」往「推論」延伸，這會讓晶片種類變多、數量變大，進而同時推升 IC 載板
的量與規格。此前從 Deep Learning 、Perceptual AI 一路到 Generative AI、Agentic AI，未來再往
Physical AI 發展。訓練（Learning）主要依賴大型模型運算，因此核心晶片以高效能 GPU 為主；
但到了推論（Inference）階段，更重視即時反應速度、功耗與成本，因此除了 GPU 之外，CPU 與
ASIC 的使用比例會明顯提高。展望 2026/2027 年，預計南電載板稼動率持續改善，其中 ABF 與
BT 均已進入高檔運作。ABF 方面，台灣樹林及錦興廠稼動率已接近滿載；昆山廠則為 2H26 稼動
率回升的主要來源，受惠美系網通客戶及中國客戶量產，ABF 稼動率由 2Q26 約 50%快速提升至
3Q26 的 60%–70%，預期 4Q26 在供給持續吃緊下，可進一步提升，2027 年初亦將因應美系客戶
Server CPU 需求快速成長。BT 載板供需則更加緊張，除現有 DRAM、SSD 相關需求持續強勁，
另一方面，部分同業逐步將 BT 產能轉向 ABF，韓系供應商亦優先支應集團內需求，市場有效供給
持續收縮，景碩亦表示稼動率也將逐步向上，預期 2027 年 BT 仍將維持高稼動率。 
綜合上述，本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1095.4 億元，年增 49.3%/82.6%，毛利率
26.9%/43.4%，營業利益 141.3/442.3 億元，年增 612.7%/213.1%，稅後獲利 117/366.9 億元，年
增 501.2%/213.5%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第5 頁，共 19頁  
產業概況  
AI 需求增溫造成 T-glass 玻纖布供應吃緊，助益近期 BT 載板報價調整 
低熱膨脹係數（Low CTE ）玻璃纖維布，簡稱 T-glass，為高階 AI ABF 載板及高階 BT 載板重要原
料，由 Nittobo(日東紡)獨家供應，並由日系廠商 Resonac(ABF 載板 CCL 主要供應商)、三菱瓦斯
(BT 載板 CCL 主要供應商)採用。由於高階 AI 晶片需求增溫帶動高階 AI ABF 載板出貨成長，造成
T-glass 供應吃緊並連帶使高階 BT 載板 CCL(終端用途包含記憶體控制器、手機 Ap、Sip 等)交貨
期拉長，T-glass 玻纖布供應正常時高階 BT 載板 CCL 交貨期約 8-10 週，後因供應吃緊造成交貨期
拉長至 16-20 週，目前因玻纖布供應更為緊張已無告知交貨期。 
經供應鏈調查，台系載板廠已於 2025 年 7 月開始針對部分採用 T-glass 之 BT 載板產品調漲價格，
此外 BT 載板亦有載板廠進行價格調漲，以反應高階玻纖布供應日漸緊缺，以及年初以來銅價、金
價等原物料成本上漲壓力。由於 BT 載板產業長期以來供過於求，且當前高階 AI 晶片普遍採用
ABF 載板，多數載板廠此前 BT 業務毛利率較為偏低，淡季時季毛利率多有承壓。本中心認為漲價
趨勢可延續，將有助載板廠獲利改善。

圖 4：高階 BT 載板供應鏈示意圖

資料來源：元大投顧整理

圖 5：銅價近一年走勢

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第6 頁，共 19頁

圖 6：金價近一年走勢

資料來源：Cmoney、元大投顧整理

中長期高階 ABF 載板價格或能進一步調升 
受惠 AI 發展浪潮帶動 AI GPU 、AI ASIC 等 AI 晶片需求成長，AI ABF 載板需求正向。本中心推測
短期高階 AI ABF 載板因獲利水準足夠暫無調漲壓力，但中長期隨需求持續上升、載板面積增大、
T-glass 供應持續吃緊，AI ABF 載板產品台系廠商後續仍有進一步議價空間。目前欣興、南電、景
碩皆有耕耘高階 ABF 載板，例如欣興在美系 GPU Blackwell 市佔率預估為 30-40%，且為全球主要
AI ASIC 載板供應商；南電今年 800G 載板比重預期約 7%，包含美系、非美系等終端需求；景碩
則有望成為美系 AI GPU 載板第三供。此外部分載板廠因原物料上漲等成本壓力在 2Q25、3Q25 小
幅調整中低階 ABF 載板報價，本中心判斷若 T-glass 供應持續吃緊並排擠 E-glass 產能，則後續中
低階 ABF 載板價格有望進一步提升。

圖 7：高階 ABF 載板供應鏈示意圖

資料來源：元大投顧整理

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第7 頁，共 19頁  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 91%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic) 、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國四大 CSP 資本支出將年增 91%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA GB300 的天下，AWS、Google、
Meta ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將
採購 TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，本中心預估 2026 年通用型伺服器出貨量將年增
23%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP 等業
者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中將消
耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 8

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第8 頁，共 19頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，NVIDIA
加 AMD GPU 預估年增 17%及 34%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用
GPU 算力需求仍維持成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者， 2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下， CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本， ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

圖 9：GPU/ASIC 出貨量預估

資料來源：元大投顧

AI 晶片面積持續擴大，將使 ABF 載板用量大增 
隨著大型語言模型與生成式 AI 的算力需求快速攀升，AI 晶片的設計持續朝向更高運算密度與更大
面積發展。無論是 NVIDIA、AMD 或各家大廠的新世代加速器，都呈現晶粒尺寸擴大、功耗上升、
I/O 腳位數倍增的趨勢。在此背景下，ABF 載板的需求不僅以「量」擴張，更以「規格」升級為主
軸。為支撐大型 GPU/AI ASIC 的高頻寬連接與多晶片架構，ABF 層數、線寬線距、孔徑控制及翹
曲管理均需同步提升，帶動高端載板技術門檻不斷拉高。由於先進 AI 晶片的封裝面積更大、電源
與訊號整合更複雜，單顆晶片所需 ABF 載板面積與材料用量明顯增加。整體而言，AI 晶片規模擴
大正驅動 ABF 產業進入新一輪升級周期，高階需求將成為未來成長的主要動能。

### Page 10

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第10 頁，共 19頁  
獲利調整與股票評價  
給予南電買進評等，目標價 1700 元 
本中心維持 2026–2027 年載板廠商將持續受惠於高稼動率與漲價趨勢的看法不變。主因新世代高
階載板規格持續大型化，傳統 PC／NB 載板尺寸多約 27×27mm，而 Server CPU 、AI ASIC 及高
速交 換器 載板 已逐 步 放大 至 70–80mm 、100mm 以 上， 部分 大型 產品 甚 至 朝 140×140mm 、
150×150mm 發展，未來更大型先進封裝架構更可能逼近 200mm 等級。由於載板生產係以固定尺
寸 Panel 進行製造，隨單顆載板面積放大，每張 Panel 可排入的產品數量將快速下降，同時邊角無
法有效利用所造成的 Area Loss 亦明顯增加，此將持續消耗載板廠商有效產能，有助產業維持高稼
動率，並支撐 2026–2027 年載板報價向上。本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1095.4 億元，
年增 49.3%/82.6%，毛利率 26.9%/43.4%，營業利益 141.3/442.3 億元，年增 612.7%/213.1%，
稅後獲利 117/366.9 億元，年增 501.2%/213.5%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。 
綜合上述，南電高階產品比重提升、稼動率維持高檔及報價上調帶動下，預期 ABF／BT 毛利率可
望持續改善，整體營運動能於 2027 年明顯增強。評價方面，南電歷史本益比交易區間約介於 6–36
倍；考量本輪 ABF／BT 供需結構改善、高階產品組合升級，以及稼動率快速提升所帶來的毛利率
擴張，本中心給予 2027 年 30 倍本益比評價，以 2027 年預估 EPS 推算目標價 1,700 元，並給予
買進投資建議。

公司簡介  
南亞電路板成立於 1997 年，前身為南亞塑膠自 1985 年開始營運的電路板事業部，1998 年正式獨立營運，並於 2006 年在
台灣證券交易所掛牌上市，為台塑集團旗下主要 PCB 與 IC 載板廠商，目前南亞塑膠持股約 67%。南亞電路板     年報
_8046 南電_2025 年報(1) 公司產品涵蓋傳統多層印刷電路板、HDI、軟硬複合板（Rigid-Flex），以及 ABF 與 PP／BT IC
載板，生產據點主要位於台灣錦興、樹林及中國昆山。南亞電路板 其中 IC 載板為公司主要高階產品布局，應用涵蓋伺服器
CPU、AI ASIC、高效能運算（HPC）、800G／1.6T 高速交換器、記憶體、SSD 控制晶片、SiP、行動裝置及車用電子等
領域；一般 PCB 則持續往 AI 伺服器、GPU 加速卡、伺服器與高階網通等高附加價值產品升級。    年報_8046 南電_2025
年報(1) 公司近年營運重心進一步向 AI 與 HPC 相關高階載板移動，受惠 AI 晶片朝大尺寸、高層數及高 I/O 規格發展，以及
CSP 持續擴大自研 ASIC 布局，南電持續強化高階 ABF 載板、1.6T 交換器及 AI 伺服器相關產品布局，並規劃新增高階載
板產能，以支應下一階段 AI 基礎建設需求。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 南電整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低等風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於略為領先
的位置，在電子零組件行業的公司中排名略微領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 南電的整體曝險屬於低等水準，略為領先電子零組件行業的平均水準。  
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：南電在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。公司訊息揭露能力非常優良，
提供投資人及市場充足的資訊。

證券發行機構之看法；(2) 研究部分析師於本研究報告中所提出之特定投資建議或觀點，與其過去、現在、未來薪酬的任何部份皆無直接或間接關聯。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

목표주가 (하향) 165,000 원 
현재주가 108,600 원 
상승여력 51.9 %

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

Document 148: AI交易忧虑压垮美股，纳指跌超2%、CoreWeave暴跌12%，VIX一度升破22，布油涨破110

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI交易忧虑压垮美股，纳指跌超2%、CoreWeave暴跌12%，VIX一度升破22，布油涨破110.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

芯⽚指数⼋连跌，美科技七巨头指数单⽇下挫 1.6% ，本周累跌 2.87% 。存储概念指数当天
跌 6.28% 。存储芯⽚股 SK 海⼒⼠ ADR 跌超 6.5% ，闪迪跌超 6.8% ，美光科技跌超 5.6% 。光
通信公司 Coherent 跌逾 7% 。 10 年期美债收益率扭转三连降。美元指数创逾两周新低，离
岸⼈⺠币盘中再创四年新⾼。布油盘中涨超 3% ，美油七⽇连涨。
摘要：
科技股遭新⼀轮抛售， AI 投资盈利能⼒的疑虑与油价骤升形成双重压⼒，美股周五全线下挫。纳
指单⽇重挫 2% ，芯⽚股连续第⼋个交易⽇收跌，创下 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录。
周五，纳斯达克 100 指数收跌 2.5% ，标普 500 指数跌 1.1% 。云计算服务商 CoreWeave 单⽇暴跌
12% ，其债券收益率本周⼀度突破 15% ，信⽤⻛险攀升⾄通常预⽰财务困境的⽔平。
本周， AI 交易的核⼼⽭盾正从算⼒需求增⻓转向融资成本、资本开⽀回报和企业盈利能⼒。云服
务商 CoreWeave 债务承压，进⼀步引发市场对 AI 数据中⼼建设融资模式的审视；存储芯⽚、光

通信和数据中⼼设备等此前受益于 AI 投资热潮的股票，也成为资⾦减仓的重点。媒体指出，
CoreWeave 股价持续下跌令市场更关注 AI 交易的融资问题，未来⼏周科技巨头的财报将检验⾼
估值能否得到盈利⽀撑。
在能源层⾯，布伦特原油结算价收于每桶约 110 美元附近， WTI 原油单⽇涨幅达 3.8% ，报每桶
101.71 美元。美国两党议员联合提出法案，拟阻⽌特朗普政府与莫斯科达成的俄罗斯柴油⼊市
协议。
AI 盈利能⼒遭质疑，芯⽚股抛压持续
AI 基础设施投⼊能否产⽣⾜够回报，已成为市场新的核⼼争议。
周五美股开盘涨跌不⼀，随后卖压逐渐扩⼤。道指⾼开后早盘⼀度上涨超过 460 点、涨幅接近
0.9% ，但午盘转跌，跌幅⼀度接近 310 点、约 0.6% 。标普 500 指数微幅⾼开后转跌，午盘跌幅⼀
度接近 1.2% ；纳斯达克综合指数低开低⾛，收盘跌幅扩⼤⾄ 2.15% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

其他指数中，纳斯达克 100 指数跌 2.5% ，⽣物科技指数⼀度跌超 2.5% ，费城半导体指数跌超
3.5% 。芯⽚股持续⾛弱，凸显市场对 AI 投资回报和融资模式的疑虑，已经从个别公司蔓延⾄整
个产业链。
AI 产业链股票成为抛售重灾区。存储概念指数当天跌 6.28% 。存储芯⽚股SK 海⼒⼠ ADR跌超
6.5% ，闪迪跌超 6.8% ，美光科技跌超 5.6% 。
光通信公司Coherent跌逾 7% 。为 AI 数据中⼼建设投⼊巨资的甲⻣⽂盘中跌超 4% 。 AI 指数表现
远逊于剔除 AI 股的标普指数。

### Page 3

科技七巨头遭遇重挫。万得科技七巨头单⽇下挫 1.6% ，本周累跌 2.87% 。
嘉盛的 Fawad Razaqzada 表⽰：
CoreWeave 作为 AI 算⼒基础设施的早期⼊局者，⼤量依赖⾼收益债务市场为扩张融资，在当前
利率⾼企的背景下处境尤为脆弱。 Razaqzada 指出，相关⻛险已开始向信贷市场蔓延。 " 这对 AI
相关交易形成了两难困境 —— 利率⾛⾼既压低未来现⾦流的现值，同时⼜推⾼了⽀撑 AI 下⼀阶段
增⻓所需基础设施的融资成本。 "
据彭博社报道，以超⼤规模云服务商为代表的科技巨头年度资本⽀出正逼近 1 万亿美元，其中⼤
部分来⾃债务融资。彭博⾏业研究数据显⽰，尽管最⼤型科技公司多持有投资级信⽤评级，但
基⾦经理正对 CoreWeave 等垃圾级借款⽅提出越来越多的质疑。
并不是所有⼈都愿意跟着砍估值。⾼盛的 Rich Privorotsky 认为市场在局部反应过度：代币需求
仍在超预期增⻓，纳指的前瞻市盈率已跌到约 21 倍，为本轮 AI 周期启动以来最低。

美股资⾦轮动⾄防御与能源板块
本周的价格⾛势表明，市场的讨论重⼼正在转移。问题已不再是 AI 的市场空间有多⼤，⽽是实现
这⼀前景所需的巨额投⼊最终能否带来利润。

### Page 4

美股必需消费板块逆势收红，百事可乐、亿滋国际、好时同步⾛⾼，科技七巨头⾥苹果是唯⼀
收涨的⼀只，涨幅 0.43% 。
能源股是油价回升最直接的受益者。埃克森美孚当天预告炼油业务的利润将远超预期，柴油价
格处在历史⾼位正是原因，它与雪佛⻰的涨幅都排在标普 500 前列。
莫德纳是当天标普 500 ⾥跌幅最⼤的⼀只，盘中⼀度跌超 12% ，收盘跌 7.82% 。美国⻝品药品监
督管理局拒绝受理其季节性流感疫苗 mRNA-1010 的上市申请，给出的理由是三期临床试验缺少
“ 充分且严格对照 ” 的研究设计。
分化在周线层⾯看得更清楚。本周道指涨 0.68% ，纳指跌 4.48% ，两者周度表现差超过 6 个百分
点，为 2002 年 7 ⽉以来最⼤；当⽇恰逢⽉度期权到期， VIX 盘中⼀度升破 22 ，为四个⽉来⾸次。
沃什讲话未强化 10 ⽉加息预期
周五早盘， 9 ⽉进出⼝物价与⼯业产出数据公布后， 10 年期美债收益率⼀度上⾏ 3.8 个基点⾄
5.125% ；到收盘，这⼀涨幅收窄到不⾜ 1 个基点，收益率报 5.097% ，⽇内最⾼触及 5.133% 。

本周来看， 2 年期、 5 年期、 10 年期和 30 年期收益率分别下⾏ 3.8 个、 12.4 个、 13.4 个和 15.5 个基
点，⻓端明显跑赢短端，曲线⾛成⽜平形态。就⼀周⽽⾔，美国国债迎来了 2 ⽉以来最好的表
现。

### Page 5

沃尔什在曼⾕与国际货币基⾦组织总裁格奥尔基耶娃对话时拒绝表态，只说 “ 我不预判会议 ” ，并
称两次会议之间⼤体符合美联储的预期。投资者把它读成了 10 ⽉不会再动。
他还补充说，他不相信应该暗⽰ 2026 年剩余时间⾥他会采取什么⾏动，同时坚称美联储 “ 已经⾮
常明确地表⽰，我们将实现价格稳定 ” 。真正影响市场的是他评估道：
分析认为，对于⼀个在 9 ⽉份加息并预计今年还会加息⼀次的美联储来说，这番话听起来像是委
婉地暗⽰ 10 ⽉份会议上不会加息。
本周，尽管市场对 2026 年加息的预期基本持平（尽管存在波动），但对2027 年加息的预期却明
显下滑。
在两次会议之间，世界经济总体上符合我们的预期。

### Page 6

⾼盛⾸席经济学家 Jan Hatzius 团队写道，把沃尔什的表态与鲍曼的 “ 我没有紧迫感 ” 、戴利不愿
凭有限信息做决定放在⼀起看，联邦公开市场委员会将在 10 ⽉暂停，年内第⼆次加息留给 12
⽉。
⾼盛政府债券交易主管 Andrew Mullen 的说法更直⽩。市场多年来第⼀次真正⽆视⽕热的通胀数
据，避险买盘回来了， “ 如果⼀定要我说，这轮⾏情还有后劲 ” 。
美联储内部已经有⼈承认了这件事。理事沃勒本周早些时候表⽰，收益率上⾏已经在替美联储
分担⼀部分⼯作。也就是说紧缩不必完全靠加息完成，信⽤市场⾃⼰就能把⾦融条件收紧。
美元指数创逾两周新低，离岸⼈⺠币再创四年新⾼
汇市⽅⾯，美元指数周五⾛弱，势将结束此前连续四周上涨的⾛势。 ICE 美元指数（ DXY ）连续
两⽇盘中跌破 102.00 ，在周五欧股盘中跌⾄ 101.58 ，连续两⽇刷新 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇以来低位，⽇内跌近
0.5% 。
美元⾛弱推动⼈⺠币继续上⾏，离岸⼈⺠币（ CNH ）兑美元连续两⽇盘中涨破 6.69 ，美股早盘

曾逼近 6.6830 ，连续两⽇刷新 2022 年 7 ⽉以来盘中⾼位。
本周三和周四先后公布的美国 CPI 和 PPI 通胀数据⾼于预期，⼀度推动交易员增加对美联储进⼀
步加息的押注；但沃什周五的讲话没有强化 10 ⽉加息预期，叠加美债收益率此前回落，美元的
利率优势有所减弱。

### Page 7

欧元兑美元从近期低位回升，美元兑⽇元则在 157.9 附近波动。整体⽽⾔，市场正在重新权衡美
国通胀韧性、未来利率路径与 AI 投资⻛险。若科技股抛售进⼀步加剧，避险需求可能继续影响主
要货币⾛势，但美元能否恢复上涨仍取决于美国利率预期和全球⻛险偏好的变化。
布油盘中涨超 3% ，美油七连涨，⻩⾦企稳于 4100 美元上⽅
原油⽅⾯，国际油价周五盘中有所回落，但仍处⾼位。布伦特原油此前因中东供应⻛险⽽上
涨，周五盘中⼀度在 105 美元上⽅交易；美国 WTI 原油则在每桶 97 美元附近波动。
美股早盘传出美国两党众议员提出议案阻⽌特朗普的俄罗斯柴油松绑协议后，原油加速上⾏，
美股午盘之初，美国 WTI 原油曾接近 100.90 美元，⽇内涨近 3% ，布伦特原油曾逼近 109.50 美
元，⽇内涨逾 3.5% 。
市场继续关注霍尔⽊兹海峡周边紧张局势，以及相关事件对全球原油运输和供应的影响。
油价维持⾼位也令宏观市场难以完全摆脱通胀担忧。能源成本上升可能推⾼整体通胀，并使美
联储更难迅速转向宽松；但在周五的交易中，投资者对 AI 产业链融资⻛险的关注，同样主导了股

票市场的⽅向。
到周五纽约时段尾盘，现货⻩⾦报 4,168.69 美元，全天跌 0.05% ，⼏乎收平。 12 ⽉加息选项仍然
留在桌⾯上，这是压制它反弹空间的主要原因。现货⽩银报 56.85 美元，跌 0.70% 。

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
本周⻩⾦和⽩银⾛势疲软。本周三，受消费者物价指数（ CPI ）公布后实际收益率飙升的影响，
⻩⾦价格跌⾄两个⽉低点，⽩银价格也暴跌约 8% ，但随着收益率回落，⾦银价格均有所回升。
本周⽐特币价格基本持平。本周三跌破 82000 美元，周四在 80000 美元附近获得⽀撑，最终维持
在 81000 美元左右。

### Page 9

Document 165: Anthropic信用评级曝光：BB+，最高级别的垃圾级信用

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic信用评级曝光：BB+，最高级别的垃圾级信用.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic ⾸获信⽤评级 BB+ ，最⾼级别的投机级（垃圾级）评分，仅⽐投资级低⼀档。评
级机构 KBRA 在信披极为有限的情况下，以 " ⻛险评分 " 替代完整财务模型完成评估。但
KBRA 相较于穆迪、标普和惠誉体量更⼩，⽽后三者将最终决定 Anthropic 上市后的正式信
⽤评级。
摘要：
AI 独⻆兽 Anthropic 的信⽤评级⾸次浮出⽔⾯。
据英国《⾦融时报》获得的⽂件显⽰，信⽤评级机构 Kroll Bond Rating Agency （ KBRA ）于今
年 6 ⽉授予 Anthropic BB+ 发⾏⼈评级 —— 即最⾼级别的投机级（垃圾级）评分，仅⽐投资级低
⼀档。该评级在 Anthropic 完成⼀笔由 Apollo 主导、规模达 350 亿美元的芯⽚融资交易之前获得，
此前从未公开披露。
这⼀评级对 Anthropic 的融资前景具有直接影响。华尔街各⼤银⾏正在筹备⼀笔 600 亿美元的新债
务⽅案，以帮助 Anthropic 收购更多由⾕歌和博通联合开发的定制 AI 芯⽚，其中包括 180 亿美元的

次级债务。能否跨越投资级⻔槛，将决定 Anthropic 能否以更低成本接触寿险公司、养⽼基⾦等
更⼴泛的机构投资者群体。
评级细节：有限信披下的 " ⻛险评分 "
KBRA 授予的 BB+ 评级附有稳定展望，针对的是 350 亿美元融资包中约 44 亿美元的次级债务 ——
这部分债务将投资者直接暴露于 Anthropic 的信⽤⻛险之下；其余部分则由芯⽚制造商博通提供
担保。该笔次级债以约 8.5% 的收益率发⾏，⾼级债的利率则为 5.75% 。
这⼀评级是在信息披露相当有限的情况下作出的。
据⽂件显⽰， KBRA 获得的财务信息包括营收、调整后营业收⼊以及 2026 年第⼀季度年化运营
收⼊，以及 Anthropic 提供的第⼆季度业绩内部预测，但⾃由现⾦流及 EBITDA 等关键财务指标均
未纳⼊披露范围。评级分析师不得不依赖假设⾃⾏估算，并最终采⽤ " ⻛险评分 " ⽅式替代传统的
完整财务模型来评估其信⽤状况。
财务表现：增速放缓，但⾼于预期
Anthropic 在周三举⾏的 IPO 前投资者推介会上披露，第三季度收⼊环⽐增⻓近 50% ⾄ 168 亿美

元，并连续两个季度实现调整后盈利。
不过，这⼀增速较年初有所放缓，最新财务数据也低于部分最乐观的市场预期。
在评级决定作出时， Anthropic 曾预测第⼆季度收⼊为 109 亿美元，据知情⼈⼠透露，实际数字⾼
于预期，达到 115 亿美元。截⾄ 6 ⽉底，这家成⽴五年的公司⾃⾝资产负债表上债务为零，此前
⼀直依靠信⽤状况更佳的业务合作伙伴及特殊⽬的载体进⾏融资。
据知情⼈⼠透露， Anthropic 还可能发⾏最多 420 亿美元的可转换票据，⽤于⽀付向博通租赁半导
体的费⽤，但 KBRA 未获提供该可转换票据的详细条款。
评级竞赛：三⼤机构才是最终裁判
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

KBRA 相较于穆迪、标普和惠誉体量更⼩，⽽后三者将最终决定 Anthropic 上市后的正式信⽤评
级。
Anthropic 及竞争对⼿ OpenAI 的银⾏顾问已游说上述主要评级机构，争取在两家公司上市后给
予投资级评定。投资者预计 OpenAI 的 IPO 估值将超过 2 万亿美元。
能否获得投资级评级，对 Anthropic 的⻓期融资战略⾄关重要。该公司预计未来⼗年将在计算资
源和基础设施上投⼊逾 5000 亿美元，这⼀庞⼤的资本开⽀计划也被视为⽀撑美国股市投资热潮
的重要⽀柱之⼀。

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

Source: Bloomberg
Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.

Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe

in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in

in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of

Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

ACES Industrial product portfolio Industrial sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

Document 221: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 12-16

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 12-16.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 12-16
Date:    9 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 12-16

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 12-16
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 11 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week was relatively quiet on the economic data front, but the
indicators released pointed to continued economic strength. ADP weekly
payroll gains increased for the fifth consecutive week, reaching a three-
month high, while jobless claims fell to multi-year lows. The ISM NM-PMI
remained solid at 54.9 in September, though its prices paid component
jumped to 74.0, the highest since July 2022. Redbook same-store sales
rose 8.3% y/y during the week of October 2, signaling continued strength

in consumer spending, while the New York Fed's consumer survey
showed expected spending growth and one-year-ahead inflation
expectations both reaching three-year highs.
The data suggest that the Fed has good reason to be hawkish.
Accordingly, September's FOMC meeting minutes showed that most
policymakers favored another rate hike by year-end, with several judging
monetary policy to be barely restrictive, if at all. We agree that further
tightening is warranted. However, recent comments by New York Fed

President John Williams, Fed Vice Chair Philip Jefferson, and Governor
Christopher Waller suggest flexibility on the timing. The markets assign a
17.2% probability to an October hike and a 71.6% probability of a
December hike.
This week's inflation reports could change those odds. A hotter-than-
expected CPI reading on Wednesday could prompt the markets to price in
an October hike, pressuring the Fed after September's unanimous 25bps
increase, which Fed Chair Kevin Warsh described as removing "a dose of

accommodation." An in-line or cooler CPI reading would support patience.
Warsh speaks Thursday evening at the IMF and World Bank annual
meetings in Bangkok.
Here's more:

### Page 4

(1) Inflation. The September CPI and PPI reports will be released on
Wednesday and Thursday, respectively. The Cleveland Fed’s Inflation
Nowcast model projects a hot 0.53% m/m increase in headline CPI, while
core CPI is expected to rise a more modest 0.2%. That would put headline
and core CPI inflation at 3.6% and 2.4% y/y, respectively. September is
expected to mark the 67th consecutive month of CPI inflation exceeding
2.0% y/y, underscoring the persistence of inflationary pressures (chart).

Adding to inflation concerns, the averages of the prices-paid and prices-
received indexes of five regional Fed banks’ business surveys rose to their
highest levels since 2022 in September (chart). Both have historically
tracked headline CPI inflation closely.

### Page 5

The same indicators have also closely tracked PPI final demand inflation,
which was already running hot at 5.4% y/y in August (chart).
(2) Monthly Treasury statement. The federal budget deficit totaled $1.77
trillion over the 12 months through August, while outstanding marketable
Treasury securities increased by $2.12 trillion over the 12 months through
September (chart). The recent slowdown in Treasury borrowing suggests
that the deficit may have narrowed somewhat. Tuesday's report will reveal

the final budget deficit for fiscal 2026, which ended on September 30.
(3) Consumer spending. Thursday's September retail sales report will
provide the first official reading on consumer spending for the month. We
expect another solid increase, as Redbook same-store sales rose 8.3%
y/y in September, well above the 2025 average of 5.8%. Redbook sales

### Page 6

historically have tracked official retail sales closely, suggesting that
consumer spending maintained strong momentum through the end of Q3
(chart).
(4) Manufacturing. The week will also provide important insights into the
manufacturing sector, with the October New York and Philadelphia Fed
banks’ business surveys due Thursday, followed by September's industrial
production and capacity utilization reports on Friday.
Manufacturing aggregate weekly hours rose to 84.4 in September, the

highest since August 2023, continuing their recent upward trend (chart).
Immediate expensing under the OBBBA, reshoring, and the AI buildout
are helping to reinvigorate manufacturing activity. With skilled labor in
short supply, manufacturers appear to be meeting stronger demand partly
by increasing the hours worked by existing employees. The rise in
aggregate hours worked suggests that manufacturing production
expanded at a healthy pace in September.

### Page 7

The New York and Philadelphia Fed manufacturing surveys provide
another reason for optimism. Their average manufacturing PMI rose to
22.7 in September, the highest since 2021 (chart). Historically, the
average has closely tracked manufacturing production growth, suggesting
that factory output could accelerate further in the months ahead.

Document 276: MSCI、トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、格下げの可能性を警告 ～トルコ株式市場の混乱は長期化の様相、MSCIは改革の進捗を注視～

Source PDF: all report pdf/MSCI、トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、格下げの可能性を警告 ～トルコ株式市場の混乱は長期化の様相、MSCIは改革の進捗を注視～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 16 日

1/3 
MSCI、トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、格下げの可能性を警告 
～トルコ株式市場の混乱は長期化の様相、MSCI は改革の進捗を注視～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ トルコ株式市場では、 投資信託による株式売却圧力を背景に下落基調が続いている。MSCI は
６月、一部投資信託による低流動性銘柄への保有集中や浮動株比率の歪みに懸念を示し、当
局に情報開示や市場監視の強化を求めた。背景には、政府が国内資金をリラ建て資産に誘導
したことで、低浮動株企業の上場や投資信託への資金流入が拡大し、低流動性銘柄の株価上
昇と資金流入が相互に増幅する構造が形成されたことがある。 
○ その後、資本市場委員会（ＳＰＫ）が投資信託の保有上限を厳格化し、年末までに超過分の
解消を求めたことで資金循環が逆回転した。株価下落に伴う解約増加から、一部運用会社が
解約請求に応じられない事態となり、現金確保を目的に流動性の高い銘柄にも売りが波及し
た。 当局は資金供給の拡充や投資信託の取引停止・清算のほか、 関係者を刑事告発するなど、
早期の事態収拾に向けた取り組みを強化している。 
○ MSCI は10 月15 日、 トルコ株式指数の変更を凍結し、2027 年６月までに十分な市場改革が進
まなければ、新興国市場からフロンティア市場などへの格下げを検討する可能性を示した。
格下げとなればパッシブ投資家を中心に資金流出が加速する懸念がある。加えて、利下げ観
測に伴うリラ安から家計資金が外貨や金に流れる可能性もあり、市場混乱の長期化が実体経
済や政府の政策運営に波及するリスクに注意が必要である。

トルコ株の下落基調が続いている（図１）（注１）。背景には、現金確保を目的に投資信託が株式
の売却圧力を強めていることがある。今年６月、指数算出会社である MSCI が一部の投資信託によ
る株式保有の集中や、流動性の低い銘柄に関する警告を発したことがきっかけとなった。 
具体的には、特定の中小上場企業と密接な関係にある投資信託の保有分が、浮動株比率の推計値
を人為的に押し上げていると、海外の機関投資家から懸念が寄せられていることを明らかにした。
こうした懸念は、当該銘柄の指数構成比率を歪めることにつながる。

注１ 10 月９日付レポート「トルコ金融市場の混乱が収まらない」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666443.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
このため、同社は当局に対して、実質
的な所有者に関する詳細かつ適時の情
報開示のほか、 協調的な取引行動に対す
る強固な監視と取り締まり、 構造的に歪
められた浮動株を特定するための透明
性が高いルールに基づく枠組みの構築
を求める考えを示した。そのうえで、11
月に実施する指数見直しまでに十分か
つ具体的な進展が見られない場合、 同国
と対象銘柄の適切な取り扱いについて
協議を開始する可能性があるとした。 
こうした懸念が高まった背景には、2023 年に財務相に返り咲いたシムシェキ氏の下、 同国政府が
国内貯蓄をリラ建て資産にとどめる取り組みを進めたことがある。そのため、2021 年に導入された
為替保護型預金（ＫＫＭ：リラ安による為替下落率が預金金利を上回った場合、その差額を政府・
中銀が補填する制度）を廃止し、通常のリラ建て預金への移行を促進したほか、株式や投資信託な
どにも資金を誘導した。 
一方で、同国では高金利状態が長期化しており、政府は企業に借入ではなく、株式市場の活用に
よる資金調達の積極化を推進したため、浮動株比率が低い企業が数多く上場した。こうした銘柄は
市場で売買可能な株式数が限られ、株価が大きく変動しやすいことで、価格形成に歪みが生じた。
さらに、 その後の投資ブームを追い風に投資信託が急拡大し、 投資家にとっては株式投資の間口が
広がる一方、リスクの集中が懸念された。具体的には、投資信託に組み入れられる比較的流動性が
低い銘柄への資金流入が活発化し、 株価上昇に伴い運用収益が向上した結果、 新たな資金流入を生
む循環が形成された。 
規制機関である資本市場委員会（ＳＰＫ）は８月、投資信託の純資産に対する組入比率、発行済
株式数に対する保有割合など保有ポジションの上限を厳格化し、2026 年末までに超過分の解消を
義務付ける規制変更を発表した。具体的には、10 月末までに超過分の３分の１、11 月末までに３
分の２、12 月末までに完全解消を求めている。 
その結果、 株価を押し上げてきた資金流入の循環が逆回転し、 株価下落により投資信託の解約請
求が膨らんだ。 さらに、 複数の資産運用会社が投資家による解約請求に応じられないと発表する事
態に発展した。 流動性の低い保有資産の売却が困難になり、 現金確保を目的に流動性の高い銘柄に
売却圧力が強まったことで、市場全体に売り圧力が広がっている。 
市場の混乱を受けて、 中銀は流動性確保を目的とするレポ資金の供給額引き上げのほか、 銀行間
資金市場における銀行の借入限度額も大幅に引き上げた。ＳＰＫも、複数の投資信託の取引停止・
清算を命じたほか、関係者を刑事告発するといった動きをみせている。 
こうしたなか、MSCI は 10 月 15 日、トルコ株式指数に関する変更を凍結することを明らかにし
た。当局による市場改革を評価するためにさらなる時間が必要とし、2027 年６月までに十分な進展

### Page 1

We've managed to get through a week without mapping out
new multi-decade highs in various long yields and spreads.
That's great, but we'd not get too comfortable. Many of the
drivers of prior stresses remain in play. And muddy
developments in the Ukraine and Iran wars make for diﬃcult
interpretation – enough to keep us all on edge, at least
Padhraic Garvey, CFA
The mood for US Treasuries has calmed.
But don't get too comfortable
Long-end yields have calmed in the past week or so. But pressures

remain. The medium-term issuance narrative stemming from ﬁscal
largesse remains. That’s the 'bad'. Then there’s the 'ugly', in the
guise of energy price pressure stemming from the Iran war. That
has coincided with higher printed inﬂation. Arguably, this is the
biggest direct impact, as it has prompted central bank rate hikes.
And there is the 'good'. Higher real rates have been an important
driver, and a part of this likely incorporates AI-driven productivity

growth. If that last part was the central driver, we’d have little to

of long-end weakness still to play out. This could take the US 10-
year yield back into the 5.25% to 5.5% range. Other markets would
likely move in tandem, mapping out similar, albeit less dramatic,
blow-outs. With 5.25% looking like a mean-reversion level, we think
the US 10-year yield could overshoot towards 5.5% and remain

elevated for a number of months as bond market jitters persist.
This could quite easily extend into the ﬁrst quarter of 2027.
For later in 2027, we think inﬂation will come down, and long
rates should too. On the growth side, we have a 2% real
growth forecast tendency for 2027. If we’re wrong and we’re
on the verge of a macro boom (and/or persistently elevated
inﬂation), that’s a whole other story, and it would be a route
to 5.5-6%. The other way to get there could be the US ﬁscal

story ﬁnally unravelling in a bad way, but we aren't quite
sure we can call for that yet as a baseline view.
For 2027, we have growth of 2% and inﬂation of 2% (or
slightly above on both counts). That points to 4.75%,
representing a mild upside bias relative to the 4.5% level we
view as broadly neutral. But ﬁrst, we need to get through
2026.
The big French widening
